    "originalFile": "\\section{Problem Setup and Methods}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}\n\\label{sec:commitment-point}\n\nWhen a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \\textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases. Steps before CP are \\emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \\emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The detection task produces a score $s_t \\in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.\n\nWe study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\\cref{tab:models}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Arch.} & \\textbf{Params} & \\textbf{Layers} & \\textbf{Hidden} & \\textbf{$n$ (total\\,/\\,test)} \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-8B & Llama & 8B & 32 & 4096 & 209\\,/\\,43 \\\\\nOT-7B & Qwen & 7B & 28 & 3584 & 228\\,/\\,47 \\\\\nR1-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 100\\,/\\,20 \\\\\nQwQ-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 95\\,/\\,19 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Models studied. R1-8B and R1-32B are distilled from DeepSeek-R1~\\citep{deepseekr1}; QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwq} is a native reasoning model; OT-7B~\\citep{openthinker} is trained with STILL~\\citep{still} on Qwen2.5-7B. Data: HarmThoughts~\\citep{harmthoughts} with step-level CP annotations. Split: 60/20/20.}\n\\label{tab:models}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Probe Architecture}\n\\label{sec:probe-architecture}\n\n\\paragraph{Hidden-state (HS) probes.} At each step $t$, we extract the last-token hidden state $\\mathbf{h}_t^{(\\ell)} \\in \\mathbb{R}^d$ from layer $\\ell$ and train an L2-regularized logistic regression classifier (balanced class weights, $C{=}1.0$, LBFGS, max\\_iter${}=2000$) on binary CP labels. Sweeping $C \\in [0.01, 100]$ yields $<$2pp BA variation (\\cref{sec:experiments}), confirming robustness to this default. Layer selection is analyzed in \\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}.\n\n\\paragraph{Text probes.} Each step's text is encoded with all-MiniLM-L6-v2~\\citep{wang2020minilm} ($\\mathbb{R}^{384}$), a distilled sentence encoder from the Sentence-BERT family~\\citep{reimers2019sentence}. The same logistic regression is applied as a surface-level modality baseline.\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal windowing.} For window size $W$, we concatenate features from steps $[t{-}W{+}1, \\ldots, t]$. We evaluate $W \\in \\{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\\}$ and select per-model on validation. GRU sequence models are also evaluated under two protocols: a 70/30 train/test split (primary) and the same 60/20/20 split used for LR experiments (for fair comparison; \\cref{tab:gru_multi_seeds,app:gru_details}).\n\n\\subsection{Layer Selection Sensitivity}\n\\label{sec:layer-sensitivity}\n\nLayer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. We evaluate five metrics: balanced accuracy (BA), precision, and inverse FPR are standard probing metrics~\\citep{belinkov2022probing}; crossing rate and additive composite come from the safety detection literature. A multiplicative composite (BA $\\times$ crossing rate) serves as the \\emph{crossing-rate composite} (CR-composite) baseline, so named because it incorporates crossing rate in the selection criterion. Metrics evaluating probe performance at a fixed decision threshold (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) form the \\emph{threshold-based} selection. This classification is an empirical observation from metric disagreement, not an a priori design choice; the ablation in \\cref{tab:layer_selection} is a reproducible empirical fact independent of our framing. Crossing rate inflates at shallow layers where representations are compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\\times$ smaller; \\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), so threshold crossings reflect noise rather than signal. This compression is an independently verifiable geometric property of shallow-layer representations, not an artifact of probe training. Precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite all derive from the same $2\\times2$ confusion matrix and therefore converge to the same layer by mathematical necessity; BA is threshold-free and provides independent confirmation. The ablation diagnoses which \\emph{aspect} drives layer selection bias. The effective number of independent tests is four (one per model), reflected in our HB correction. Results in \\cref{sec:layer-results}; full per-layer sweeps in \\cref{app:layer_sweep}.\n\nWe acknowledge a garden-of-forking-paths concern~\\citep{gelman2013garden}: the CR-composite vs.\\ threshold-based distinction was identified during analysis, not pre-registered, and the confirmatory analyses (random-CP decomposition, cosine volatility; \\cref{app:random_cp,app:cosine_volatility}) were developed post-hoc. However, the five-metric ablation (\\cref{tab:layer_selection}) is a reproducible empirical fact, and the mechanism (shallow-layer compression inflating crossing rate) is independently verifiable from representation geometry.\n\n\\subsection{Baseline Methods}\n\\label{sec:baselines}\n\nWe evaluate seven alternatives to contextualize probe performance (\\cref{tab:negative}): BOCPD~\\citep{adams2007bayesian} and CUSUM~\\citep{page1954cusum} for change-point detection; Mahalanobis distance following TrajGuard~\\citep{trajguard}; RepE mean-difference directions~\\citep{zou2023representation}; two-layer MLP probes; CCS-adapted~\\citep{burns2023discovering}; and cross-model transfer via PCA. A position-only baseline ($t/T$) controls for temporal artifacts. Detailed configurations in \\cref{app:bocpd_config,app:transfer_details}.\n\n\\subsection{Evaluation Protocol}\n\\label{sec:evaluation}\n\nWe report balanced accuracy, precision, pre-CP FPR, coverage, and lead time. We exclude F1, AUROC, and MCC: F1 conflates precision and recall (our coverage metric already captures recall-like behavior at the trace level); AUROC is threshold-free and cannot diagnose the layer selection bias that manifests at fixed decision thresholds; MCC is redundant with BA under balanced class priors and adds no discriminative information for layer selection. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings, selected on the R1-8B validation set and held fixed across all models as the value balancing coverage ($\\geq$70\\%) and safe-trace false positive rate ($\\leq$50\\%); \\cref{tab:k_ablation} confirms that the HS vs.\\ text conclusion direction is consistent at $K{=}3$, $5$, and $7$, with coverage and FPR trading off monotonically. Modality comparisons use BCa bootstrap (10{,}000 resamples, resampling at the step level within each trace; traces with more reasoning steps contribute proportionally more observations)~\\citep{efron1993bootstrap,diciccio1996bootstrap} with Holm-Bonferroni step-down (3 metrics $\\times$ 4 models $= 12$ tests, $\\alpha{=}0.05$); the 4-test framework yields identical conclusions (\\cref{app:holm_bonferroni}). For 32B models ($n \\leq 20$), the exact permutation test is the primary inference method; BCa confidence intervals are reported for comparability but may have reduced coverage at these sample sizes. Within each model, the three metrics are positively correlated, so the correction is stricter than under independence. Control task selectivity~\\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine structure (\\cref{app:control}). $\\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \\leq 20$): R1-32B ($n{=}20$) and QwQ-32B ($n{=}19$).\n",

For 32B models ($n \\leq 20$), the exact permutation test is the primary inference method; BCa confidence intervals are reported for comparability but may have reduced coverage at these sample sizes. Within each model, the three metrics are positively correlated, so the correction is stricter than under independence. Control task selectivity~\\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine structure (\\cref{app:control}). $\\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \\leq 20$): R1-32B ($n{=}20$) and QwQ-32B ($n{=}19$).\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 4,
        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 4,
        "newLines": 7,
        "lines": [
          " \\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}",
          " \\label{sec:commitment-point}",
          " ",
          "-When a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \\textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases. Steps before CP are \\emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \\emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The detection task produces a score $s_t \\in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.",
          "+When a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \\textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases; traces with oscillating safety deliberation are resolved by taking the final irreversible transition (\\cref{sec:limitations}). Steps before CP are \\emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \\emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The detection task produces a score $s_t \\in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.",
          " ",
          " We study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\\cref{tab:models}).",
          " "
        ]
      }

          " \\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\\label{sec:layer-results}",
          " ",
          "-\\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\\% depth) for QwQ-32B and L10 (16\\%) for R1-32B. Three of four metrics excluding crossing rate (precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite) consistently select deep layers at L63 (98\\%) for both 32B models. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\\% depth under 60/20/20; \\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}), itself demonstrating that layer selection is a consequential methodological choice. The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine distances are 1.4--2.7$\\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). A linear probe in this compressed space operates near the noise floor, generating frequent threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power (\\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}).",

However, QwQ-32B's advantage is fully absorbed by the quadratic term (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), suggesting its residualized advantage contains a non-linear position component. This sensitivity, combined with R1-32B's non-significance under linear control, indicates that 32B position mediation results should be interpreted cautiously.           " ",
          "-The downstream impact is severe: at CR-composite layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage; both 32B models fail. At threshold-based layers, the HS advantage is graded: confirmed at 7--8B (OT-7B, R1-8B; nested CV validated), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$, and exploratory at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (residualized gap below MDE). The $\\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \\leq 20$) where effect sizes are descriptive rather than confirmatory. Had we reported only CR-composite-layer results, we would have concluded a scale-dependent HS advantage that is entirely a methodological artifact. A random-label control on QwQ-32B confirms that the crossing-rate shallow-layer bias is a geometric property of the representations (100\\% of random-label repeats select shallow layers), while threshold-based deep-layer selection requires real safety labels (\\cref{app:random_cp}). AUROC, excluded from our primary metrics because it is threshold-free (\\cref{sec:evaluation}), confirms HS discriminability at BA-selected layers ($\\Delta$AUROC +12.1 to +15.1pp; \\cref{tab:ba_layer_results}) but cannot diagnose the threshold-dependent layer selection bias.",

          "-The HS advantage is encoder-conditional: against BGE-large (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB correction (\\cref{app:encoder_robustness}). Multi-seed stability appears in \\cref{app:multi_seed}. Against the 384d baseline, HS probes outperform text probes in all four models$^\\dagger$ (\\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}): confirmed at 7--8B (OT-7B: +21.9pp precision, R1-8B: +20.1pp), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (+15.4pp, position-mediated). R1-8B FPR is directionally consistent (5/5 seeds) but not robust: $p{=}0.044$ (rank 12/12 in the HB step-down, the weakest surviving test), with only 1/5 seeds passing Bonferroni correction (\\cref{app:multi_seed}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds ($\\Delta$Prec std${=}2.0$pp) but its advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \\cref{app:position_residual}); QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB (\\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\\cref{app:control}); selectivity measures label structure (main vs.\\ shuffled), complementary to position contribution (temporal artifacts). Position features account for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy (\\cref{app:position_residual}); R1-32B's HS advantage is fully explained by position (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$).",
          "+The HS advantage is encoder-conditional: against BGE-large (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB correction (\\cref{app:encoder_robustness}). Multi-seed stability appears in \\cref{app:multi_seed}. R1-8B FPR is directionally consistent (5/5 seeds) but not robust: $p{=}0.044$ (rank 12/12 in the HB step-down, the weakest surviving test), with only 1/5 seeds passing Bonferroni correction (\\cref{app:multi_seed}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds ($\\Delta$Prec std${=}2.0$pp) but its advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \\cref{app:position_residual}); QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB (\\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\\cref{app:control}); selectivity measures label structure (main vs.\\ shuffled), complementary to position contribution (temporal artifacts). Position features account for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy (\\cref{app:position_residual}); R1-32B's HS advantage is fully explained by position (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$).",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (79--92\\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$, all HB). Two individual metrics are non-significant: R1-8B BA (+1.3pp, $p{=}0.731$) and QwQ-32B FPR ($-$11.4pp, $p{=}0.038$, below HB threshold). The control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation but does not rule out representation quality differences. Random projection to 384d yields similar results ($\\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp; \\cref{app:random_projection}), further weakening the capacity explanation. E5-Mistral-7B (4096d$\\rightarrow$PCA 384d) yields intermediate results (2/4 HB; \\cref{app:e5_mistral}), forming a monotonic encoder-quality gradient with MiniLM (4/4) and BGE-large (1/4).",

These methods assume threats from external input manipulation and operate at the token level on instruction-tuned models.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}\nReasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.\n\n\\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\\cite{selfjailbreak}. H-CoT~\\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\\cite{chen2025unfaithful,turpin2023language} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff}, a phenomenon our temporal probes target. Recent work further documents adversarial reasoning capabilities and information leakage in reasoning traces~\\cite{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak,hu2026refusal_dynamics,ahrend2026safer_traces}, providing additional motivation for hidden-state monitoring.\n\n\\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} trains a dedicated reasoning-based guard model.\n\n\\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.\n\n\\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}\nInternal activations can detect safety violations in standard LLMs. \\citet{azaria2023internal} demonstrate that internal states encode whether a model's output is truthful, motivating representation-based monitoring. \\citet{zhou2024alignment} show safety-relevant information distributes across layers, while \\citet{qi2025shallow_alignment} find that alignment operates primarily over early output tokens. \\citet{arditi2024refusal} identify a single refusal direction, but \\citet{zhao2025harmfulness} show harmfulness and refusal are encoded separately. \\citet{joad2026refusal_multi} reveal eleven category-specific refusal directions, and \\citet{wollschlager2025geometry} formalize multi-dimensionality through concept cones, consistent with our finding that single-direction methods are insufficient. TrajGuard~\\cite{trajguard} uses Mahalanobis distance from safe trajectories; SafeSwitch~\\cite{safeswitch} trains hidden-state classifiers; SIREN~\\cite{siren} monitors internal representations during generation; SafeDream~\\cite{safedream2026} proposes proactive world models for detection. These methods assume threats from external input manipulation and operate at the token level on instruction-tuned models. The self-jailbreak setting differs because transitions are internal and span multiple reasoning steps. \\citet{kadali2025internal} decompose internal layer representations to separate jailbreak from benign prompts; their results confirm that layer-wise structure carries safety-relevant signal. We reproduce TrajGuard (\\cref{sec:negative}) and find its geometric approach fails because discriminative signal resides in minor PCA components.\n\n\\paragraph{Probing Methodology and Layer Selection.}\nCausal abstraction~\\cite{geiger2025causal} formalizes when high-level causal models faithfully describe neural computation, grounding use of internal representations. Linear probing spans layer-wise classifiers~\\cite{alain2017understanding}, sentence probing~\\cite{conneau2018probing}, and surveys~\\cite{belinkov2019analysis,belinkov2022probing,rogers2020primer}. Control tasks~\\cite{hewitt2019control} distinguish structure from memorization (we adopt these; \\cref{app:control}). Information-theoretic and causal formulations address probe complexity~\\cite{voita2020information,pimentel2020information,ravfogel2020null,ravfogel2022rlace,belrose2023leace,elazar2021amnesic}. \\citet{marks2024geometry} find emergent linear structure in LLM truth representations, consistent with our safety commitment separability. That information distributes non-uniformly across layers is well established: \\citet{tenney2019bert} show syntactic information concentrates in early layers while semantic information peaks later, and \\citet{jawahar2019bert} confirm this hierarchy through probing and attention analysis. Our contribution is orthogonal: the known layer-wise information gradient interacts with metric choice to produce systematic selection bias in safety probes, a phenomenon not previously characterized. Most directly, \\citet{boxo2025linear_probes} show linear probes can rely on textual evidence rather than deeper representations; our metric bias analysis provides a complementary mechanism: shallow-layer probes exploit compressed representations that inflate crossing rate, a geometric effect consistent with text-leakage at layers where representations have not yet diverged from surface features. \\citet{alvarez2026transport_geometry} apply hidden-state geometry to step-level hallucination detection concurrently with our work. Despite this foundation, layer selection remains underexamined: most studies select by a single metric without sensitivity analysis, a practice we show can reverse conclusions (\\cref{sec:layer-results}). Our approach is correlational; causal validation~\\citep{geiger2025causal,belrose2023leace,li2024iti} is needed to confirm probed directions are causally active.\n\n\\paragraph{Alternative Detection Approaches.}\nBOCPD~\\cite{adams2007bayesian} detects abrupt distributional shifts; CCS~\\cite{burns2023discovering} discovers latent truth directions via consistency constraints; RepE~\\cite{zou2023representation} identifies concept directions through mean differences. We adapt all three to temporal commitment detection (\\cref{sec:negative}). BOCPD and CUSUM fail because commitment is gradual. CCS provides no gain because its consistency objective misaligns with temporal structure. RepE shows inconsistent performance across models (0.756 on QwQ, 0.559 on R1-32B). These failures motivate supervised probes with principled layer selection as the minimal effective methodology.\n",

    "oldString": "Despite this foundation, layer selection remains underexamined: most studies select by a single metric without sensitivity analysis, a practice we show can reverse conclusions (\\cref{sec:layer-results}). Our approach is correlational; causal validation~\\citep{geiger2025causal,belrose2023leace,li2024iti} is needed to confirm probed directions are causally active.",
    "newString": "Despite this foundation, layer selection remains underexamined: most studies select by a single metric without sensitivity analysis, a practice we show can reverse conclusions (\\cref{sec:layer-results}). \\citet{pais2026linear_probe_scaling} show that multi-layer ensembling recovers performance where single-layer probes fail, a complementary solution to the sensitivity problem we diagnose. Our approach is correlational; causal validation~\\citep{geiger2025causal,belrose2023leace,li2024iti} is needed to confirm probed directions are causally active.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}\nReasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.\n\n\\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\\cite{selfjailbreak}. H-CoT~\\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\\cite{chen2025unfaithful,turpin2023language} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff}, a phenomenon our temporal probes target. Recent work further documents adversarial reasoning capabilities and information leakage in reasoning traces~\\cite{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak,hu2026refusal_dynamics,ahrend2026safer_traces}, providing additional motivation for hidden-state monitoring.\n\n\\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} trains a dedicated reasoning-based guard model.\n\n\\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.\n\n\\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}\nInternal activations can detect safety violations in standard LLMs. \\citet{azaria2023internal} demonstrate that internal states encode whether a model's output is truthful, motivating representation-based monitoring. \\citet{zhou2024alignment} show safety-relevant information distributes across layers, while \\citet{qi2025shallow_alignment} find that alignment operates primarily over early output tokens. \\citet{arditi2024refusal} identify a single refusal direction, but \\citet{zhao2025harmfulness} show harmfulness and refusal are encoded separately. \\citet{joad2026refusal_multi} reveal eleven category-specific refusal directions, and \\citet{wollschlager2025geometry} formalize multi-dimensionality through concept cones, consistent with our finding that single-direction methods are insufficient. TrajGuard~\\cite{trajguard} uses Mahalanobis distance from safe trajectories; SafeSwitch~\\cite{safeswitch} trains hidden-state classifiers; SIREN~\\cite{siren} monitors internal representations during generation; SafeDream~\\cite{safedream2026} proposes proactive world models for detection; HiddenDetect~\\cite{jiang2025hiddendetect} extends hidden-state monitoring to vision-language models; SafeInt~\\cite{safeint2025} applies representation intervention for defense; RCS~\\cite{rcs2026} uses Mahalanobis-based contrastive scoring for jailbreak detection. These methods assume threats from external input manipulation and operate at the token level on instruction-tuned models. The self-jailbreak setting differs because transitions are internal and span multiple reasoning steps. \\citet{kadali2025internal} decompose internal layer representations to separate jailbreak from benign prompts; their results confirm that layer-wise structure carries safety-relevant signal. We reproduce TrajGuard (\\cref{sec:negative}) and find its geometric approach fails because discriminative signal resides in minor PCA components.\n\n\\paragraph{Probing Methodology and Layer Selection.}\nCausal abstraction~\\cite{geiger2025causal} formalizes when high-level causal models faithfully describe neural computation, grounding use of internal representations. Linear probing spans layer-wise classifiers~\\cite{alain2017understanding}, sentence probing~\\cite{conneau2018probing}, and surveys~\\cite{belinkov2019analysis,belinkov2022probing,rogers2020primer}. Control tasks~\\cite{hewitt2019control} distinguish structure from memorization (we adopt these; \\cref{app:control}). Information-theoretic and causal formulations address probe complexity~\\cite{voita2020information,pimentel2020information,ravfogel2020null,ravfogel2022rlace,belrose2023leace,elazar2021amnesic}. \\citet{marks2024geometry} find emergent linear structure in LLM truth representations, consistent with our safety commitment separability. That information distributes non-uniformly across layers is well established: \\citet{tenney2019bert} show syntactic information concentrates in early layers while semantic information peaks later, and \\citet{jawahar2019bert} confirm this hierarchy through probing and attention analysis. Our contribution is orthogonal: the known layer-wise information gradient interacts with metric choice to produce systematic selection bias in safety probes, a phenomenon not previously characterized. Most directly, \\citet{boxo2025linear_probes} show linear probes can rely on textual evidence rather than deeper representations; our metric bias analysis provides a complementary mechanism: shallow-layer probes exploit compressed representations that inflate crossing rate, a geometric effect consistent with text-leakage at layers where representations have not yet diverged from surface features. \\citet{alvarez2026transport_geometry} apply hidden-state geometry to step-level hallucination detection concurrently with our work. Despite this foundation, layer selection remains underexamined: most studies select by a single metric without sensitivity analysis, a practice we show can reverse conclusions (\\cref{sec:layer-results}). Our approach is correlational; causal validation~\\citep{geiger2025causal,belrose2023leace,li2024iti} is needed to confirm probed directions are causally active.\n\n\\paragraph{Alternative Detection Approaches.}\nBOCPD~\\cite{adams2007bayesian} detects abrupt distributional shifts; CCS~\\cite{burns2023discovering} discovers latent truth directions via consistency constraints; RepE~\\cite{zou2023representation} identifies concept directions through mean differences. We adapt all three to temporal commitment detection (\\cref{sec:negative}). BOCPD and CUSUM fail because commitment is gradual. CCS provides no gain because its consistency objective misaligns with temporal structure. RepE shows inconsistent performance across models (0.756 on QwQ, 0.559 on R1-32B). These failures motivate supervised probes with principled layer selection as the minimal effective methodology.\n",

None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.\n\n\\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}\nInternal activations can detect safety violations in standard LLMs. \\citet{azaria2023internal} demonstrate that internal states encode whether a model's output is truthful, motivating representation-based monitoring. \\citet{zhou2024alignment} show safety-relevant information distributes across layers, while \\citet{qi2025shallow_alignment} find that alignment operates primarily over early output tokens. \\citet{arditi2024refusal} identify a single refusal direction, but \\citet{zhao2025harmfulness} show harmfulness and refusal are encoded separately. \\citet{joad2026refusal_multi} reveal eleven category-specific refusal directions, and \\citet{wollschlager2025geometry} formalize multi-dimensionality through concept cones, consistent with our finding that single-direction methods are insufficient. TrajGuard~\\cite{trajguard} uses Mahalanobis distance from safe trajectories; SafeSwitch~\\cite{safeswitch} trains hidden-state classifiers; SIREN~\\cite{siren} monitors internal representations during generation; SafeDream~\\cite{safedream2026} proposes proactive world models for detection; HiddenDetect~\\cite{jiang2025hiddendetect} extends hidden-state monitoring to vision-language models; SafeInt~\\cite{safeint2025} applies representation intervention for defense; RCS~\\cite{rcs2026} uses Mahalanobis-based contrastive scoring for jailbreak detection. These methods assume threats from external input manipulation and operate at the token level on instruction-tuned models. The self-jailbreak setting differs because transitions are internal and span multiple reasoning steps. \\citet{kadali2025internal} decompose internal layer representations to separate jailbreak from benign prompts; their results confirm that layer-wise structure carries safety-relevant signal. We reproduce TrajGuard (\\cref{sec:negative}) and find its geometric approach fails because discriminative signal resides in minor PCA components.\n\n\\paragraph{Probing Methodology and Layer Selection.}\nCausal abstraction~\\cite{geiger2025causal} formalizes when high-level causal models faithfully describe neural computation, grounding use of internal representations. Linear probing spans layer-wise classifiers~\\cite{alain2017understanding}, sentence probing~\\cite{conneau2018probing}, and surveys~\\cite{belinkov2019analysis,belinkov2022probing,rogers2020primer}. Control tasks~\\cite{hewitt2019control} distinguish structure from memorization (we adopt these; \\cref{app:control}). Information-theoretic and causal formulations address probe complexity~\\cite{voita2020information,pimentel2020information,ravfogel2020null,ravfogel2022rlace,belrose2023leace,elazar2021amnesic}. \\citet{marks2024geometry} find emergent linear structure in LLM truth representations, consistent with our safety commitment separability. That information distributes non-uniformly across layers is well established: \\citet{tenney2019bert} show syntactic information concentrates in early layers while semantic information peaks later, and \\citet{jawahar2019bert} confirm this hierarchy through probing and attention analysis. Our contribution is orthogonal: the known layer-wise information gradient interacts with metric choice to produce systematic selection bias in safety probes, a phenomenon not previously characterized. Most directly, \\citet{boxo2025linear_probes} show linear probes can rely on textual evidence rather than deeper representations; our metric bias analysis provides a complementary mechanism: shallow-layer probes exploit compressed representations that inflate crossing rate, a geometric effect consistent with text-leakage at layers where representations have not yet diverged from surface features. \\citet{alvarez2026transport_geometry} apply hidden-state geometry to step-level hallucination detection concurrently with our work. Despite this foundation, layer selection remains underexamined: most studies select by a single metric without sensitivity analysis, a practice we show can reverse conclusions (\\cref{sec:layer-results}). \\citet{pais2026linear_probe_scaling} show that multi-layer ensembling recovers performance where single-layer probes fail, a complementary solution to the sensitivity problem we diagnose. Our approach is correlational; causal validation~\\citep{geiger2025causal,belrose2023leace,li2024iti} is needed to confirm probed directions are causally active.\n\n\\paragraph{Alternative Detection Approaches.}\nBOCPD~\\cite{adams2007bayesian} detects abrupt distributional shifts; CCS~\\cite{burns2023discovering} discovers latent truth directions via consistency constraints; RepE~\\cite{zou2023representation} identifies concept directions through mean differences. We adapt all three to temporal commitment detection (\\cref{sec:negative}). BOCPD and CUSUM fail because commitment is gradual. CCS provides no gain because its consistency objective misaligns with temporal structure. RepE shows inconsistent performance across models (0.756 on QwQ, 0.559 on R1-32B). These failures motivate supervised probes with principled layer selection as the minimal effective methodology.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample-dependent. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}\nThe HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmed at 7--8B; descriptive at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders (e.g., LLM-based embeddings~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral}) could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.\n\n\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard linear position control, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under stricter quadratic control, only the two 7--8B models remain significant (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), consistent with the exploratory status of 32B results. We propose---but have not validated---a multi-metric audit protocol for layer selection: compute $\\geq$3 metrics (threshold-free and threshold-based), flag metric disagreement, and verify cosine volatility at candidate layers; details and requirements in \\cref{app:cosine_volatility}. Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\\% FPR at 21\\% coverage; \\cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). Three of four models share the Qwen architecture; broader validation across architectures, datasets, and attack types is needed before deployment.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}\nThe HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmed at 7--8B; descriptive at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Under standard linear position control, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under stricter quadratic control, only the two 7--8B models remain significant (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), consistent with the exploratory status of 32B results. Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\\% FPR at 21\\% coverage; \\cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). {
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/conclusion.tex",
  "old_string": "Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The HS advantage is confirmed at 7--8B (nested CV validated) but carries model-specific caveats at 32B$^\\dagger$ (position mediation, limited coverage, descriptive sample sizes) and narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.",
  "new_string": "Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\\dagger$ are exploratory (position mediation, limited coverage, descriptive sample sizes). The advantage narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). These findings provide initial characterization on one benchmark; Contribution~1 (layer selection sensitivity) generalizes from representation geometry, while Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) requires validation on additional datasets. Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps."
}

    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Hyperparameter selection and scope.}\nLayer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class at 32B, up to $2{,}240$ configurations); cumulative degrees of freedom are substantial",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Hyperparameter selection and scope.}\nLayer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class at 32B, up to $2{,}240$ configurations). HB correction covers the final 12 model-metric tests but not the cumulative researcher degrees of freedom in layer, metric, window, and evidence tier selection; cumulative degrees of freedom are substantial",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Limitations}\\label{sec:limitations}\n\n\\paragraph{Single dataset.}\nAll experiments use one dataset (HarmThoughts~\\citep{harmthoughts}), which provides step-level CP annotations derived from manual inspection of reasoning chains. The dataset spans multiple harmful categories (e.g., violence, fraud, illegal activities) but we do not stratify results by category; categories with longer average reasoning chains or earlier CPs may exhibit different probe dynamics. CP positions range from early to late within traces, though the distribution is right-skewed (most CPs occur in the first half of reasoning). Annotation granularity (step-level vs.\\ token-level), category distribution, and annotator team composition may all influence which layers carry discriminative signal and how strongly metric bias manifests. Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations. Random annotation noise would attenuate effect sizes symmetrically, making our estimates conservative; systematic noise (e.g., consistent early or late CP placement) could bias layer selection or inflate modality differences. Without IAA, we cannot distinguish these cases, so our conclusions may not transfer to independently annotated corpora. Generalization to other jailbreak datasets, attack taxonomies, or multilingual settings is untested.\n\n\\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}\nCurrent probes characterize the commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors: false positive rates remain 11--50\\% (model-dependent), and the 76.7\\% oracle gap (R1-8B; \\cref{sec:oracle}) underscores how much room remains. R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\\% coverage, 5/20 traces) and its HS advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \\cref{app:position_residual}). QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\\ 0.796) with negative HS lead time. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). At $n{=}19$--$20$, the minimum detectable effect at 80\\% power is 8.6--9.5pp (\\cref{app:power_analysis}); all 32B conclusions marked $\\dagger$ should be interpreted with this constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameter selection and scope.}\nLayer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class at 32B, up to $2{,}240$ configurations); cumulative degrees of freedom are substantial (\\cref{app:hyperparameter_robustness} provides convergence, cross-validation, and bootstrap analyses addressing this concern). BA-optimal layer indices are sensitive to the train/validation split, spanning 7--98\\% depth across models and splits (\\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); for example, OT-7B shifts from L16 to L2 and QwQ-32B from L60 to L63 between 70/30 and 60/20/20 splits, a direct manifestation of the layer selection sensitivity that constitutes our primary finding. Threshold-based metrics are more stable but still vary by up to 4 layers. $K{=}5$ was selected on the R1-8B validation set and held fixed (\\cref{app:k_ablation}); joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified for QwQ-32B (\\cref{app:layer_sweep}) but not R1-32B. CP annotations follow HarmThoughts~\\citep{harmthoughts} without independent IAA evaluation; if annotation error is ${\\geq}2$ steps, QwQ-32B's lead time ($-$1.2 to +1.9) falls within noise, though perturbation analysis shows smooth degradation with no cliff effects (\\cref{app:cp_perturbation}). Three of four models share the Qwen architecture; only R1-8B (Llama-based) provides cross-architecture evidence. We cover 2 architectures and 2 scales; intermediate scales, diverse base architectures, proprietary models, and multilingual settings are untested. Results are correlational; causal intervention is needed to confirm mechanistic involvement. Cross-model transfer was tested with PCA-based subspace alignment only; more sophisticated approaches (e.g., Procrustes rotation, CKA-guided projection~\\citep{kornblith2019cka}, model stitching~\\citep{bansal2021stitching}) remain future work.\n\n\\paragraph{CP permanence assumption.}\nOur binary CP definition assumes that once safety deliberation ceases, it does not resume. In practice, some reasoning traces exhibit oscillating patterns where the model intermittently returns to safety-aware reasoning after apparent commitment. The current binary framework treats the first permanent cessation as CP, but traces with oscillating safety deliberation violate this assumption and may be misclassified. A multi-state formulation (e.g., pre-commitment / wavering / committed) could better capture these dynamics but would require richer annotations and larger datasets to train reliably.\n",

QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\\ 0.796) with negative HS lead time. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). At $n{=}19$--$20$, the minimum detectable effect at 80\\% power is 8.6--9.5pp (\\cref{app:power_analysis}); all 32B conclusions marked $\\dagger$ should be interpreted with this constraint.",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01F8WxkwZvNyiebuiqJPKTET`</summary>

```text
11	Our contributions are:
12	\begin{itemize}
13	    \item \textbf{Layer selection sensitivity.} We identify a systematic bias in composite metrics that reverses HS probe conclusions at 32B scale. A five-metric ablation establishes principled selection criteria (\cref{sec:layer-results}).
14	
15	    \item \textbf{Encoder-quality boundary with evidence gradient.} Against 384d MiniLM, the HS advantage is confirmed at 7--8B (+20.1 to +21.9pp, HB corrected, nested CV validated), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (+15.4pp, position-mediated). Against 1024d BGE-large, only OT-7B survives correction (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}).
16	
17	    \item \textbf{Negative results define method boundaries.} No alternative method consistently matches supervised probing at any evidence tier (\cref{tab:negative}): BOCPD/CUSUM fail on gradual transitions; Mahalanobis distance finds near-zero separation; RepE shows inconsistent performance across models (competitive on QwQ-32B, poor elsewhere); cross-model transfer fails under PCA alignment. Control task selectivity (11.1--16.8pp above chance; \cref{app:control}) confirms genuine safety structure.
18	\end{itemize}
19	
20	\noindent These findings provide initial characterization on one benchmark for hidden-state safety monitoring and identify layer selection as a critical confound that future work must address.
```

    "newString": "the HS advantage is confirmatory at 7--8B (+20.1 to +21.9pp, HB corrected, nested CV validated), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (+15.4pp, position-mediated).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nReasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1~\\cite{deepseekr1} and QwQ~\\cite{qwq} scale inference-time computation through extended chains of thought (CoT). Recent work documents that these models can ``self-jailbreak'': without adversarial input modification, the CoT transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation~\\cite{harmthoughts,selfjailbreak}. We define the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases as the \\textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP). This failure mode differs qualitatively from adversarial jailbreaks on standard LLMs because the harmful transition originates within the model's own reasoning. Before building production detectors, a prior question must be answered: can current probing methods reliably distinguish pre-CP from post-CP reasoning steps, and under what conditions? This paper provides a systematic characterization of what hidden-state and text-based probes can and cannot detect.\n\nExisting safety detectors target fundamentally different problems. Hidden-state (HS) methods such as TrajGuard~\\cite{trajguard} and SafeSwitch~\\cite{safeswitch} classify adversarial inputs at the token level, while text monitors like SafeRemind~\\cite{saferemind} scan generated reasoning for explicit violations. Neither tracks the gradual, multi-step commitment process. Before asking which modality is better, a prior methodological question must be answered: how sensitive are probe conclusions to the choice of layer selection metric? HS probes require choosing which transformer layer to monitor, a researcher degree of freedom whose metric sensitivity has received almost no scrutiny. We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusion.\n\nWe present an initial characterization across four reasoning models---primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models), with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence---at two scales (7--8B, 32B) on the HarmThoughts benchmark. Our central finding is methodological: the choice of layer selection metric entirely determines whether an HS advantage is observed. A composite metric (balanced accuracy $\\times$ crossing rate) selects shallow layers for 32B models because shallow representations compress directional information, so a linear probe operates near the noise floor and threshold crossings reflect noise rather than semantic transitions. At these layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage.\n\nThreshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and converge by mathematical necessity; BA independently confirms deep-layer selection (\\cref{tab:layer_selection}). At these layers, the HS advantage is graded: \\emph{confirmatory} at 7--8B (precision +20.1 to +21.9pp, nested CV validated; \\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}), \\emph{significant} but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ (+19.2pp; \\cref{sec:32b-analysis}), and \\emph{exploratory} at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (+15.4pp, position-mediated, below MDE; \\cref{sec:experiments}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Layer selection sensitivity.} We identify a systematic bias in composite metrics that reverses HS probe conclusions at 32B scale. A five-metric ablation establishes principled selection criteria (\\cref{sec:layer-results}).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Encoder-quality boundary with evidence gradient.} Against 384d MiniLM, the HS advantage is confirmed at 7--8B (+20.1 to +21.9pp, HB corrected, nested CV validated), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\\dagger$ (+15.4pp, position-mediated). Against 1024d BGE-large, only OT-7B survives correction (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (\\cref{sec:32b-analysis}).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Negative results define method boundaries.} No alternative method consistently matches supervised probing at any evidence tier (\\cref{tab:negative}): BOCPD/CUSUM fail on gradual transitions; Mahalanobis distance finds near-zero separation; RepE shows inconsistent performance across models (competitive on QwQ-32B, poor elsewhere); cross-model transfer fails under PCA alignment. Control task selectivity (11.1--16.8pp above chance; \\cref{app:control}) confirms genuine safety structure.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\noindent These findings provide initial characterization on one benchmark for hidden-state safety monitoring and identify layer selection as a critical confound that future work must address.\n",

```text
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmed at 7--8B; descriptive at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders (e.g., LLM-based embeddings~\citep{wang2024e5mistral}) could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.
8	
```

    "newString": "The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B)",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}\nThe HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmed at 7--8B; descriptive at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders (e.g., LLM-based embeddings~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral}) could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.\n\n\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard linear position control, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under stricter quadratic control, only the two 7--8B models remain significant (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), consistent with the exploratory status of 32B results. Our work diagnoses the layer selection problem; \\citet{pais2026linear_probe_scaling} propose a complementary solution via multi-layer ensembling that bypasses single-layer brittleness. We propose---but have not validated---a multi-metric audit protocol: compute $\\geq$3 metrics (threshold-free and threshold-based), flag metric disagreement, and verify cosine volatility at candidate layers; details in \\cref{app:cosine_volatility}. Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\\% FPR at 21\\% coverage; \\cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). Broader validation across architectures, datasets, and attack types is needed before deployment.\n",

This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.",

          " \\paragraph{Position mediation.} Position ($t/T$) accounts for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy,\\footnote{R1-32B's 20.4pp drop reflects $W{=}1$; at $W{\\geq}3$, drops are 9--17pp.} but all models retain non-positional signal above chance (all $p{<}0.001$; \\cref{app:position_residual}). Three of four models retain significant non-positional HS advantage (residualized gap +3.7 to +6.9pp, HB corrected); R1-32B's gap is +1.9pp ($p{=}0.914$, $n{=}20$). Adding a quadratic position term $(t/T)^2$ does not change significance conclusions for the 7--8B models. However, QwQ-32B's advantage is fully absorbed by the quadratic term (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), suggesting its residualized advantage contains a non-linear position component. {

  "old_string": "R1-32B detects commitment 11--13 steps early with a +14.2pp margin over the position baseline, which confirms genuine representational signal, but covers only 25\\% of traces at the single significant window ($W{=}1$). QwQ-32B achieves statistical significance at all window sizes with 74\\% coverage, but HS probes fire later than text probes (negative lead time). Consistent with the universal position analysis (\\cref{sec:hs-text}), QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\\ 0.796). HS precision (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) margins over the position baseline are directionally positive but not statistically significant at $n{=}19$; minimum detectable effects at 80\\% power range from 8.6 to 9.5pp for 32B models (\\cref{app:power_analysis}). The divergence implies that per-model validation is essential: no single detection configuration generalizes even within the same architecture at the same scale, and a system validated on one 32B model would fail on the other.",
  "new_string": "R1-32B detects commitment 11--13 steps early (+14.2pp over position baseline) but covers only 25\\% of traces ($W{=}1$). QwQ-32B achieves significance at all window sizes with 74\\% coverage but negative HS lead time. QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher BA (0.832 vs.\\ 0.796); HS precision (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR margins are not significant at $n{=}19$; MDE at 80\\% power is 8.6--9.5pp (\\cref{app:power_analysis}). The divergence implies per-model validation is essential: no single configuration generalizes even within the same architecture at the same scale."

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}\nThe HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders (e.g., LLM-based embeddings~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral}) could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.\n\n\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard linear position control, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under stricter quadratic control, only the two 7--8B models remain significant (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), consistent with the exploratory status of 32B results. Our work diagnoses the layer selection problem; \\citet{pais2026linear_probe_scaling} propose a complementary solution via multi-layer ensembling that bypasses single-layer brittleness. We propose---but have not validated---a multi-metric audit protocol: compute $\\geq$3 metrics (threshold-free and threshold-based), flag metric disagreement, and verify cosine volatility at candidate layers; details in \\cref{app:cosine_volatility}. Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\\% FPR at 21\\% coverage; \\cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.\\looseness=-1",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}\nThe HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.\n\n\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard linear position control, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under stricter quadratic control, only the two 7--8B models remain significant (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), consistent with the exploratory status of 32B results. Our work diagnoses the layer selection problem; \\citet{pais2026linear_probe_scaling} propose a complementary solution via multi-layer ensembling that bypasses single-layer brittleness. We propose---but have not validated---a multi-metric audit protocol: compute $\\geq$3 metrics (threshold-free and threshold-based), flag metric disagreement, and verify cosine volatility at candidate layers; details in \\cref{app:cosine_volatility}. Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\\% FPR at 21\\% coverage; \\cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). Broader validation across architectures, datasets, and attack types is needed before deployment.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}\nThe HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.\n\n\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard linear position control, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under stricter quadratic control, only the two 7--8B models remain significant (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), consistent with the exploratory status of 32B results. ### Tool result

_source line 406 · 2026-05-19T15:40:06.478Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01P1v5BsUvE83qSCRZ1DhQfA`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard linear position control, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under stricter quadratic control, only the two 7--8B models remain significant (\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), consistent with the exploratory status of 32B results. Our work diagnoses the layer selection problem; \citet{pais2026linear_probe_scaling} propose a complementary solution via multi-layer ensembling that bypasses single-layer brittleness. We propose---but have not validated---a multi-metric audit protocol: compute $\geq$3 metrics (threshold-free and threshold-based), flag metric disagreement, and verify cosine volatility at candidate layers (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\% FPR at 21\% coverage; \cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). Broader validation across architectures, datasets, and attack types is needed before deployment.\looseness=-1
11	
```

</details>

We propose---but have not validated---a multi-metric audit protocol: compute $\\geq$3 metrics (threshold-free and threshold-based), flag metric disagreement, and verify cosine volatility at candidate layers (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\\% FPR at 21\\% coverage; \\cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). Broader validation across architectures, datasets, and attack types is needed before deployment.\\looseness=-1"
        ]
      }
    ],

   - Threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a 2×2 confusion matrix → converge by mathematical necessity

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}\nThe HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}; whether stronger future encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of these claims.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard linear position control, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under stricter quadratic control, only the two 7--8B models remain significant (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), consistent with the exploratory status of 32B results. Our work diagnoses the layer selection problem; \\citet{pais2026linear_probe_scaling} propose a complementary solution via multi-layer ensembling that bypasses single-layer brittleness. We propose---but have not validated---a multi-metric audit protocol: compute $\\geq$3 metrics (threshold-free and threshold-based), flag metric disagreement, and verify cosine volatility at candidate layers (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\\% FPR at 21\\% coverage; \\cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). Broader validation across architectures, datasets, and attack types is needed before deployment.\\looseness=-1\n",
        "numLines": 11,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 11
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex"
  },

        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nLayer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\\dagger$ are exploratory and the advantage narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). Contribution~1 (layer selection sensitivity) generalizes from representation geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) is initial characterization on one benchmark requiring further validation. Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.\\looseness=-1\n",
